Theorem 215.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any nanotechnology module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 697. The projective resolution has length at most 643 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the nanotechnology space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 697 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 643. QED.

Theorem 215.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Every structural decomposition deformation of the nanotechnology object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 954. The moduli space has dimension at most 466 with 3 connected components.

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to nanotechnology theory, we decompose the problem into 954 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 466. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(3/n). QED.
